\begin{array}{l} s (\delta (s)) \geq - \sum_ {g \in \delta^ {\uparrow} (s)} r _ {g} + \sum_ {\mathbf {e} \in \delta^ {\rightarrow} (s)} i _ {\mathbf {e}, s} \\ = - \sum_ {g \in \delta^ {\uparrow} (s)} r _ {g} + \sum_ {\mathbf {e} \in \delta^ {\rightarrow} (s)} \sum_ {g \in \delta^ {\uparrow} (s)} r _ {g} \frac {m _ {\mathbf {e} , s}}{\sum_ {\mathbf {f} \in \delta^ {\rightarrow} (s)} m _ {\mathbf {f} , s}} = 0. \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, we verify (v): here, we use theorem 7.1. for a good top edge  $e$  that is part of a top edge bundle  $\mathbf{f}$  we have

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ s _ {e} \right] = - \mathbb {e} \left[ r _ {e} \right] + \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {f}} \right] \frac {x _ {e}}{x _ {\mathbf {f}}} \leq - \tau p x _ {e} + (1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{6}) p \tau x _ {e} = - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{6} p \tau x _ {e}.
$$

on the other hand, for a bottom edge  $e$  with  $\mathsf{p}(e) = s$ , then

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ s _ {e} \right] = - \mathbb {e} \left[ r _ {e} \right] + \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} \right] x _ {e} \leq - \beta p x _ {e} + 0. 9 9 9 9 4 p \beta x _ {e} \leq - 0. 0 0 0 0 6 p \beta x _ {e}.
$$

<table><tr><td>name</td><td>value</td><td>set in</td><td>explanation</td></tr><tr><td>ε1/2</td><td>0.0002</td><td>global constants</td><td>half edge threshold, definition 5.12</td></tr><tr><td>ε1/1</td><td>ε1/2/12</td><td>global constants</td><td>a, b, c partitioning threshold, definition 5.18</td></tr><tr><td>p</td><td>0.005ε21/2</td><td>global constants</td><td>min prob. of happiness for a (2-* ) good edge</td></tr><tr><td>εm</td><td>0.00025</td><td>global constants</td><td>marginal errors due to max flow, definition 5.8</td></tr><tr><td>τ</td><td>0.571β</td><td>global constants</td><td>top edge decrease</td></tr><tr><td>εp</td><td>ε1/6 pτ/β</td><td>(35)</td><td>expected decrease constant, theorem 4.33</td></tr><tr><td>α</td><td>2εη</td><td>matching parameters</td><td>parameter of lemma 6.2</td></tr><tr><td>εb</td><td>21ε1/2</td><td>matching parameters</td><td>parameter of lemma 6.2</td></tr><tr><td>εf</td><td>1/10</td><td>matching parameters</td><td>parameter of lemma 6.2</td></tr><tr><td>εη</td><td>14η</td><td>(16)</td><td>definition 4.31</td></tr><tr><td>η</td><td>1/1308εp</td><td>(9)</td><td>near min cut constant</td></tr><tr><td>β</td><td>η/4.1</td><td>(8)</td><td>slack shift constant e.g. theorems 4.2, 4.24 and 4.33</td></tr></table>

table 1: a table of all constants used in the paper.

finally, we can let

$$
\epsilon_ {p} := \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{6} p \frac {\tau}{\beta} = \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 2}}{7 2} 0. 0 0 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} ^ {2} 0. 5 7 1 \geq 0. 0 0 0 0 3 9 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} ^ {3} \geq 3. 1 2 \cdot 1 0 ^ {- 1 6} \tag {35}
$$

as desired.

![](images/e52aa8eaa2f13cdb0e929bc32c636de4ea36a1e84c20861fc92790b1c9ddecba.jpg)

in the rest of this section we prove theorem 7.1. throughout the proof, we will repeatedly use the following facts proved in section 5: if a top edge  $e = (u,v)$  that is part of a bundle  $\mathbf{f}$  is reduced (equivalently  $\mathcal{h}_{\mathbf{f},u} = 1$  or  $\mathcal{h}_{\mathbf{f},v} = 1$ ), then  $u$  and  $v$  are trees, which means that tree sampling inside  $u$  and  $v$  is independent of the reduction of  $e$ .

note however, that conditioning on a near-min-cut or atom to be a tree increases marginals inside and reduces marginals outside as specified by lemma 2.23. since for any  $s \in \mathcal{h}$ ,  $x(\delta(s)) \leq 2 + \epsilon_{\eta}$ , the overall change is  $\pm \epsilon_{\eta} / 2$ .

the proof of theorem 7.1 simply follows from lemma 7.2 and lemma 7.7 that we will prove in the following two sections.

# 7.1 increase for good top edges

the following lemma is the main result of this subsection.

lemma 7.2 (top edge increase). let  $s \in \mathcal{h}$  be a degree cut and  $\mathbf{e} = (u, v)$  a good edge bundle with  $\mathsf{p}(\mathbf{e}) = s$ . if  $\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.0002$ ,  $\epsilon_{1/1} \leq \epsilon_{1/2} / 12$  and  $\epsilon_{\eta} \leq \frac{\epsilon_{1/1}}{100}$ ,  $\epsilon_f = 1/10$  then

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, u} \right] + \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \right] \leq p \tau x _ {\mathbf {e}} \left(1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{6}\right).
$$

we will use the following technical lemma to prove the above lemma.

lemma 7.3. let  $s \in \mathcal{h}$  be a degree cut with an atom  $u \in \mathcal{a}(s)$ . if  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u)) > \epsilon_{f}, \epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.0002, \epsilon_{1/1} \leq \epsilon_{1/2}/12, \epsilon_{\eta} \leq \frac{\epsilon_{1/1}}{100}$ , then we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {\substack {g \in \delta^ {\dagger} (u), \\ g \in \mathbf {f} = \left(u ^ {\prime}, v ^ {\prime}\right) \text {good top}}} \frac {1}{2} \tau x _ {g} \cdot \left(\mathbb {p} [ \delta (u) _ {t} \text {odd} | \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u ^ {\prime}} ] + \mathbb {p} [ \delta (u) _ {t} \text {odd} | \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, v ^ {\prime}} ]\right) \tag{36} \\ + \sum_ {g \in \delta^ {\uparrow} (u), \mathbf {p} (g) = s ^ {\prime} p o l y g o n} \beta x _ {g} \cdot \mathbb {p} [ \delta (u) _ {t} o d d | \mathcal {r} _ {s ^ {\prime}} ] \leq \tau (1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5}) x (\delta^ {\uparrow} (u)) f _ {u}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where recall we set  $f_{u} := 1 - \epsilon_{b}\mathbb{i}\left\{x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u))\text{is}\epsilon_{f}\text{fractional}\right\}$  in lemma 6.2, where  $\epsilon_b := 21\epsilon_{1/2}$  and  $\epsilon_f = 1/10$  as in matching parameters.

proof of lemma 7.2. by linearity of expectation and using eq. (33):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, u} \right] = \frac {m _ {\mathbf {e} , u}}{\sum_ {\mathbf {f} \in \delta^ {\rightarrow} (u)} m _ {\mathbf {f} , u}} \mathbb {e} \left[ \sum_ {g \in \delta^ {\uparrow} (u)} r _ {g} \cdot \mathbb {i} \{u \text {i s o d d} \} \right] \\ = \frac {m _ {\mathbf {e} , u}}{\sum_ {\mathbf {f} \in \delta^ {\rightarrow} (u)} m _ {\mathbf {f} , u}} \left(\sum_ {\substack {g \in \delta^ {\uparrow} (u):\\g \in \mathbf {f} = \left(u ^ {\prime}, v ^ {\prime}\right) \text {good top}}} \frac {1}{2} \tau x _ {g} \left(\mathbb {p} \left[ \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u ^ {\prime}}, \delta (u) _ {t} \text {odd} \right] + \mathbb {p} \left[ \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, v ^ {\prime}}, \delta (u) _ {t} \text {odd} \right]\right) \right. \tag{37} \\ \left. + \sum_ {g \in \delta^ {\uparrow} (u): \mathfrak {p} (g) = s ^ {\prime} \text {p o l y g o n}} \beta x _ {g} \mathbb {p} \left[ \mathcal {r} _ {s ^ {\prime}}, \delta (u) _ {t} \text {o d d} \right]\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

a similar equation holds for  $\mathbb{e}\left[i_{e,v}\right]$ .

the case where  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u)) \leq \epsilon_{f}$  or  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(v)) \leq \epsilon_{f}$  is dealt with in lemma 7.6. so, consider the case where  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u)), x(\delta^{\uparrow}(v)) > \epsilon_{f}$ . now recall that from (27),

$$
\sum_ {\mathbf {f} \in \delta^ {\rightarrow} (u)} m _ {\mathbf {f}, u} = z _ {u} x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (u)\right) \tag {38}
$$

where  $z_{u} = 1 + \mathbb{i}\left\{|s|\geq 4,x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u))\leq \epsilon_{f}\right\}$ . in this case,  $z_{u} = z_{v} = 1$

using  $\mathbb{p}[\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},u'},\delta (u)_t\mathrm{odd}] = p\mathbb{p}[\delta (u)_t\mathrm{odd}|\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},u'}]$ , and plugging (36) into (37) for  $u$  and  $v$ , we get (and using eq. (38)):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, u} \right] + \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \right] \leq p \tau \left(1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5}\right) \left(x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (u)\right) f _ {u} \frac {m _ {\mathbf {e} , u}}{x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (u)\right)} + x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (v)\right) f _ {v} \frac {m _ {\mathbf {e} , v}}{x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (v)\right)}\right) \tag {39} \\ = p \tau (1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5}) (f _ {u} m _ {\mathbf {e}, u} + f _ {v} m _ {\mathbf {e}, v}) \\ \leq p \tau (1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5}) (1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}) x _ {\mathbf {e}} <   p \tau x _ {\mathbf {e}} (1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{6}). \\ \end{array}
$$

where on the final line we used (26) and  $\epsilon_{\eta} < \frac{\epsilon_{1/1}}{100}$ .

proof of lemma 7.3. suppose that  $s_{i} \in \mathcal{h}$  are the ancestors of  $s$  in the hierarchy (in order) such that  $s_{1} = s$  and for each  $i$ ,  $s_{i+1} = \mathfrak{p}(s_{i})$ . let

$$
\delta^ {\geq i} := \delta (u) \cap \delta (s _ {i}) \qquad \text {a n d} \qquad \delta^ {i} := \delta (u) \cap \delta^ {\rightarrow} (s _ {i}).
$$

each group of edges  $\delta^i$  is either entirely top edges or entirely bottom edges. first note that if  $g\in \delta^i$  and  $g$  is a bottom edge, i.e.,  $s_{i + 1}$  is a polygon cut, then by corollary 5.10,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ \delta (u) _ {t} \operatorname {o d d} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {s _ {i + 1}} \right] = \mathbb {p} \left[ \delta (u) _ {t} \operatorname {o d d} \mid \mathcal {e} _ {s _ {i + 1}} \right] \leq 0. 5 6 7 8
$$

(see definition 5.8 and section 7 for definition of  $\mathcal{e}_{s_{i + 1}},\mathcal{r}_{i + 1})$  where in the equality we used that  $\mathcal{r}_{s_{i + 1}}$  is a uniformly random event chosen in  $\mathcal{e}_{s_{i + 1}}$ . therefore, to prove eq. (36) it is enough to show

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_{\substack{g\in \delta^{\uparrow}_{\mathrm{good}}(u):\\ g\in \mathbf{f} = (u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\text{top,}}}\frac{1}{2}\tau x_{g}(\mathbb{p}\left[\delta (u)_{t}\text{odd} | \mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},u^{\prime}}\right] + \mathbb{p}\left[\delta (u)_{t}\text{odd} |\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},v^{\prime}}\right]) \\ \leq \tau \left((1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5}) f _ {u} \left(x \left(\delta_ {\text {g o o d}} ^ {\uparrow} (u)\right) + x \left(\delta_ {\text {b a d}} ^ {\uparrow} (u)\right)\right) + 0. 0 0 1 4 x \left(\delta_ {\beta} ^ {\uparrow} (u)\right)\right) \tag {40} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we write  $\delta_{\beta}(u), \delta_{\mathrm{good}}(u), \delta_{\mathrm{bad}}(u)$  to denote the set of bottom edges, good top edges, and bad (top) edges in  $\delta(u)$  respectively and we used that

$$
\tau \left(1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5}\right) \left(1 - \epsilon_ {b}\right) - 0. 5 6 7 8 \beta \geq 0. 0 0 1 4 \tau
$$

since  $\tau = 0.571\beta$ ,  $\epsilon_{1/1} \leq \frac{\epsilon_{1/2}}{12}$ ,  $\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.0002$ , and  $\epsilon_b = 21\epsilon_{1/2}$  as defined in matching parameters.

since  $h(\mathbf{f}) \coloneqq \frac{1}{2} (\mathbb{p}[\delta(u)_t \text{ odd} | \mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},u'}] + \mathbb{p}[\delta(u)_t \text{ odd} | \mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},v'}]) \leq 1$  and  $(1 - \frac{\epsilon_{1/1}}{5}) f_u$  is nearly 1, in each of the following cases

$$
x \left(\delta_ {\beta} ^ {\uparrow} (u)\right) \geq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} 0. 0 0 3 & \text {w h e n} f _ {u} = 1 \\ \frac {4}{5} x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (u)\right) & \text {w h e n} f _ {u} = 1 - \epsilon_ {b} \end{array} \quad \text {o r} \quad x \left(\delta_ {\text {b a d}} ^ {\uparrow} (u)\right) \geq 0. 0 0 6 \quad \text {w h e n} f _ {u} \geq 1 - \epsilon_ {b}, \right. \tag {41}
$$

(40) holds. to see this, just plug in  $\epsilon_{1/1} \leq \frac{\epsilon_{1/2}}{12}$ ,  $\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.0002$ ,  $\epsilon_b = 21\epsilon_{1/2}$ ,  $\epsilon_\eta \leq 10^{-10}$ ,  $x(\delta^\uparrow(u)) \leq 1 + \epsilon_\eta$  and any inequality from (41) into (40), using the upper bound  $h(\mathbf{f}) = 1$ .

alternatively, for  $\delta_{\mathrm{top}}(u) = \delta_{\mathrm{good}}(u)\cup \delta_{\mathrm{bad}}(u)$  be the set of top edges in  $\delta (u)$ , if we can show the existence of a set  $d\subseteq \delta_{\mathrm{top}}^{\uparrow}(u)$  such that

$$
x (d) \cdot \min  _ {\substack {g \in d: \\ g \in \mathbf {f} = \left(u ^ {\prime}, v ^ {\prime}\right) \text {good}}} 1 - \frac {\mathbb {p} [ \delta (u) _ {t} \operatorname {odd} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f} , u ^ {\prime}} ] + \mathbb {p} [ \delta (u) _ {t} \operatorname {odd} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f} , v ^ {\prime}} ]}{2} \geq \left(\frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5} + 1 - f _ {u}\right) x \left(\delta_ {\text {top}} ^ {\uparrow} (u)\right), \tag{42}
$$

then, again, (40) holds.

in the rest of the proof, we will consider a number of cases and show that in each of them, either one of the inequalities in (41) or the inequality in (42) for some set  $d$  is true, which will imply the lemma.

first, let

$$
j = \max \{i: x (\delta^ {\geq i}) \geq 1 - \epsilon_ {1 / 1} \}
$$

$$
k = \max \{i: x (\delta^ {\geq i}) \geq 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} + \epsilon_ {f} / 2 \},
$$

$$
\ell = \max \{i: x (\delta^ {\geq i}) \geq 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} + \epsilon_ {1 / 1} \}
$$

just note  $j \leq k \leq \ell$ . note that levels  $\ell$  and  $k$  exist since  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u)) \geq \epsilon_{f}$ , whereas level  $j$  may not exist (if  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u)) < 1 - \epsilon_{1/1}$ ). we consider three cases:

![](images/8a79f6046802fbc65ed24b7febd95f4692db76868807dad16ab5add0d236d9be.jpg)

case 1:  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u)) \geq 1 - \epsilon_{1/1}$ : then  $j$  exists and  $s_j$  has a valid  $a, b, c$  degree partitioning (definition 5.18) where  $a = \delta(v) \cap \delta(s_j)$  such that either  $u = v$  or  $v$  is a descendant of  $u$  in  $\mathcal{h}$ . note that,  $x(\delta(u) \cap \delta(s_j)) \geq 1 - \epsilon_{1/1}$ , and by definition 5.18,  $b \cap \delta(u) = \emptyset$ . in addition, in this case,  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u))$  is not  $\epsilon_f$  fractional (see lemma 6.2), so  $f_u = 1$ .

case 1a:  $x(\delta^j) \geq 3/4$ . if  $\delta^j$  are bottom edges then (41) holds. so, suppose that  $\delta^j$  is a set of top edges. by lemma 5.25, at most  $1/2 + 4\epsilon_{1/2}$  fraction of edges in  $a \cap \delta^j$  are good but not 2-1-1 good (w.r.t.,  $u$ ). so, the rest of the edges in  $a \cap \delta^j$  are either bad or 2-1-1 good. since

$$
x (a \cap \delta^ {j}) \geq 3 / 4 - x (c) \geq 3 / 4 - 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 1} - \epsilon_ {\eta},
$$

$\delta^j$  either has a mass of  $\frac{1}{2} (1 / 4 - 2\epsilon_{1 / 1} - \epsilon_{\eta} - 4\epsilon_{1 / 2}) > 1 / 8 - 3\epsilon_{1 / 2}$  of bad edges or of 2-1-1 good edges. the former case implies that (41) holds. in the latter case, by claim 7.4 for any 2-1-1 good edge  $g \in \delta^j$  with  $g \in \mathbf{f} = (u', v')$  we have  $\mathbb{p}[\delta(u)_t \text{ odd} | \mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f}, u'}] \leq 2\epsilon_{\eta} + \epsilon_{1 / 1}$ ; so (42) holds for  $d$  defined as the set of 2-1-1 good edges in  $\delta^j$ .

case 1b:  $x(\delta^j) < 3/4$ . if  $x(\delta_\beta^\uparrow(u)) \geq 0.003$ , then (41) holds. otherwise, we apply claim 7.5 with  $\epsilon = \epsilon_{1/1}$  to all good top edge bundles  $\mathbf{f} \in d = \delta^{\geq j+1} \setminus \delta^{\geq \ell+1}$  and we get that

$$
\frac {1}{2} (\mathbb {p} \left[ \delta (u) _ {t} \mathrm {o d d} | \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u ^ {\prime}} \right] + \mathbb {p} \left[ \delta (u) _ {t} \mathrm {o d d} | \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, v ^ {\prime}} \right]) \leq 1 - \epsilon_ {1 / 1} + \epsilon_ {1 / 1} ^ {2}.
$$

since  $x(d) \geq 1 - \epsilon_{1/1} - 3/4 - 2\epsilon_{\eta} - \epsilon_{1/1} - 0.003 > 0.24$ , (42) holds.

case 2:  $1 - \epsilon_{f} < x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u)) < 1 - \epsilon_{1/1}$ . again we have  $f_{u} = 1$ . so we can either show that  $x(\delta_{\beta}^{\uparrow}(u)) \geq 0.003$  or take  $d$  to be the top edges in  $\delta^{\uparrow}(u) \setminus \delta^{\geq \ell + 1}$  and use claim 7.5 with  $\epsilon = \epsilon_{1/1}$ . this will enable us to show that (42) holds as in the previous case.

case 3:  $\epsilon_f < x(\delta^\uparrow(u)) < 1 - \epsilon_f$ : in this case  $f_u = 1 - \epsilon_b$ . if at least  $4/5$  of the edges in  $\delta^\uparrow(u)$  are bottom edges, then we are done by (41).

otherwise, let  $u' = \mathfrak{p}(u)$ . for any top edge  $e \in \delta^{\uparrow}(u)$  where  $e \in \mathbf{f} = (u'', v'')$  we have

$\mathbb{p}\left[\delta (u)_t\mathrm{odd}|\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},u^{\prime \prime}}\right]\leq \mathbb{p}\left[u'\mathrm{tree}|\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},u^{\prime \prime}}\right]\mathbb{p}\left[\delta (u)_t\mathrm{odd}|u'\mathrm{tree},\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},u^{\prime \prime}}\right] + \mathbb{p}\left[u'\mathrm{not tree}|\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},u^{\prime \prime}}\right]$

using that  $u' \subseteq u''$  is a tree under  $|\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},u''}$  with probability at least  $1 - \epsilon_{\eta} / 2$ , and applying claim 7.5 (to  $u$  and  $u'$ ) with  $\epsilon = \epsilon_f$  we have  $\mathbb{p}\left[\delta(u)_t \text{ odd}|u'\text{ tree}, \mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},u''}\right] \leq 1 - \epsilon_f + \epsilon_f^2$  we get

$$
\mathrm {p} [ \delta (u) _ {t} \text {o d d} | \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u ^ {\prime \prime}} ] \leq 1 - \epsilon_ {f} + \epsilon_ {f} ^ {2} + \epsilon_ {\eta} / 2.
$$

now, let  $d$  be all top edges in  $\delta^{\uparrow}(u)$ . then, we apply eq. (42) to this set of mass at least  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u)) / 5$ , and we are done, using that  $(\epsilon_{f} - 2\epsilon_{f}^{2}) / 5 \geq \left(\frac{\epsilon_{1/1}}{5} + \epsilon_{b}\right)$  which holds for  $\epsilon_{f} \geq 1/10$ ,  $\epsilon_{b} = 21\epsilon_{1/2}$ , and  $\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.0002$ .

claim 7.4. for  $u \in \mathcal{h}$  and a top edge  $e \in \mathbf{f} = (u', v')$  for some  $u' \in \mathcal{h}$  that is an ancestor of  $u$ , if  $x(\delta(u) \cap \delta(u')) \geq 1 - \epsilon_{1/1}$  and  $\mathbf{f}$  is 2-1-1 good, then

$$
\mathbb {p} [ \delta (u) _ {t} o d d | \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u ^ {\prime}} ] \leq 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} + \epsilon_ {1 / 1}.
$$

proof. let  $a, b, c$  be the degree partitioning of  $\delta(u')$ . by the assumption of the claim, without loss of generality, assume  $a \subseteq \delta(u) \cap \delta(u')$ . furthermore, by definition,  $b \cap \delta(u) = \emptyset$ . this means that if  $\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f}, u'} = 1$  then  $u'$  is a tree and  $a_t = 1 = (\delta(u) \cap \delta(u'))_t$  (also using  $c_t = 0$  and  $b \cap \delta(u) = \emptyset$ ). therefore,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ \delta (u) _ {t} \operatorname {o d d} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u ^ {\prime}} \right] = \mathbb {p} \left[ \left(\delta (u) \setminus \delta \left(u ^ {\prime}\right)\right) _ {t} \text {e v e n} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u ^ {\prime}} \right].
$$

to upper bound the rhs first observe that

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ (\delta (u) \setminus \delta (u ^ {\prime})) _ {t} | \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u ^ {\prime}} \right] \leq \epsilon_ {\eta} / 2 + x (\delta (u) \setminus \delta (u ^ {\prime})) \leq \epsilon_ {\eta} / 2 + x (\delta (u)) - x (a) <   1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} + \epsilon_ {1 / 1}.
$$

under the conditional measure  $|\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},u'}|, u'$  is a tree, so  $u$  must be connected inside  $u'$ , i.e.,  $(\delta(u) \setminus \delta(u'))_t \geq 1$  with probability 1. therefore,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ (\delta (u) \setminus \delta (u ^ {\prime})) _ {t} \text {e v e n} | \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u ^ {\prime}} \right] \leq \mathbb {p} \left[ (\delta (u) \setminus \delta (u ^ {\prime})) _ {t} - 1 \neq 0 | \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u ^ {\prime}} \right] \leq 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} + \epsilon_ {1 / 1}
$$

as desired.

claim 7.5. for  $u, u' \in \mathcal{h}$  such that  $u'$  is an ancestor of  $u$ . let  $\nu = \nu_{u'} \times \nu_{g / u'}$  be the measure resulting from conditioning  $u'$  to be a tree. if  $x(\delta(u) \cap \delta(u')) \in [\epsilon, 1 - \epsilon]$ , then

$$
\left. \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} [ \delta (u) \text {o d d} | (\delta (u) \cap \delta \left(u ^ {\prime}\right)) _ {t} ] \leq 1 - \epsilon + \max  \{2 \epsilon_ {\eta}, \epsilon^ {2} \}. \right. \tag {43}
$$

in other words, for any integer  $k \geq 0$ , we have  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[\delta(u) \text{ odd}|(\delta(u) \cap \delta(u'))_t = k] \leq 1 - \epsilon + \max \{2\epsilon_{\eta}, \epsilon^2\}$ .

proof. let  $d = \delta(u) \setminus \delta(u')$ . by assumption,  $u'$  is a tree, so  $d_t \geq 1$  with probability 1. therefore, since we have no control over the parity of  $(\delta(u) \cap \delta(u'))_t$

$$
\left. \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ \delta (u) _ {t} \text {e v e n} \mid (\delta (u) \cap \delta (u ^ {\prime})) _ {t} \right] \geq \min  \left\{\mathbb {p} \left[ d _ {t} - 1 \text {o d d} \mid u ^ {\prime} \text {t r e e} \right], \mathbb {p} \left[ d _ {t} - 1 = 0 \mid u ^ {\prime} \text {t r e e} \right] \right\} \right.
$$

where we removed the conditioning by taking the worst case over  $(\delta(u) \cap \delta(u'))_t$  even,  $(\delta(u) \cap \delta(u'))_t$  odd. first, observe by the assumption of the claim and that  $x(\delta^\dagger(u)) \leq 2 + \epsilon_\eta$  we have

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ d _ {t} - 1 | u ^ {\prime} \text {t r e e} \right] \in [ \epsilon , 1 - \epsilon + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} ].
$$

furthermore, since we have a sr distribution on  $g[u']$ ,  $d_t - 1$  is a bernoulli sum random variable. therefore,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ d _ {t} - 1 = 0 \mid u ^ {\prime} \text {t r e e} \right] \geq \epsilon - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}
$$

and by corollary 2.17

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ d _ {t} - 1 \text {o d d} | u ^ {\prime} \text {t r e e} \right] \geq 1 - 1 / 2 \left(1 + e ^ {- 2 \epsilon}\right) \geq \epsilon - \epsilon^ {2}
$$

as desired.

lemma 7.6. let  $s \in \mathcal{h}$  be a degree cut and  $\mathbf{e} = (u, v)$  a good edge bundle with  $\mathsf{p}(\mathbf{e}) = s$ . if  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u)) < \epsilon_{f}, \epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.0002, \epsilon_{1/1} \leq \epsilon_{1/2} / 10$ , then,

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, u} \right] + \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \right] \leq p \tau x _ {\mathbf {e}} \left(1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{6}\right)
$$

proof. first notice, by corollary 5.10 for any bottom edge  $g \in \delta^{\uparrow}(u)$  with  $\mathfrak{p}(g) = s'$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ \delta (u) _ {t} \operatorname {o d d} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \right] = \mathbb {p} \left[ \delta (u) _ {t} \operatorname {o d d} \mid \mathcal {e} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \right] \leq 0. 5 6 7 8,
$$

using  $0.5678\beta \leq \tau$  and  $f_{u} = 1$  (as  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(u))\leq \epsilon_f$ ) we can write,

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, u} \right] \leq \sum_ {h \in \delta^ {\uparrow} (u)} x _ {h} p \tau f _ {u} \cdot \frac {m _ {\mathbf {e} , u}}{z _ {u} x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (u)\right)}. \tag {44}
$$

secondly, if  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(v))\geq \epsilon_{f}$ , applying (36) and (37) to  $i_{\mathbf{e},v}$  and using  $z_{v}\geq 1$  we get

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \right] \leq \frac {m _ {\mathbf {e} , v}}{\sum_ {\mathbf {f} \in \delta^ {\rightarrow} (v)} m _ {\mathbf {f} , v}} p \tau \left(1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5}\right) x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (v)\right) f _ {v} = m _ {\mathbf {e}, v} p \tau \left(1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5}\right) f _ {v} \tag {45}
$$

case 1:  $|\mathcal{a}(s)| = 3$ , where  $\mathcal{a}(s) = \{u, v, w\}$ . let  $\mathbf{f} = (u, w)$ ,  $\mathbf{g} = (v, w)$  (and of course  $\mathbf{e} = (u, v)$ ). we will use the following facts below:

![](images/2ed076b8a6f960de2a1346d40d10c5aaf5ec217a24aa41a26dcbc7d5014d29c4.jpg)

$$
x _ {\mathbf {e}} + x _ {\mathbf {f}} \geq 2 - \epsilon_ {f} \quad (x (\delta (u)) \geq 2 \text {a n d} x (\delta^ {\uparrow} (u)) \leq \epsilon_ {f})
$$

$$
x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (v)\right) + x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (w)\right) \geq 2 - \epsilon_ {f} \quad \left(x \left(\delta (s)\right) \geq 2\right)
$$

$$
x _ {\mathbf {f}}, x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (w)\right) \leq 1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}, \tag {lemma 2.7}
$$

so we have,

$$
x _ {\mathbf {e}}, x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (v)\right) \geq 1 - \epsilon_ {f} - \epsilon_ {\eta}. \tag {46}
$$

now we bound  $\mathbb{e}\left[i_{\mathbf{e},u}\right] + \mathbb{e}\left[i_{\mathbf{e},v}\right]$ . by eq. (44) and eq. (45) (which we may apply to  $\mathbb{e}\left[i_{\mathbf{e},v}\right]$  since  $x(\delta^{\dagger}(v)) \geq \epsilon_f$ ),

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, u} \right] + \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \right] \leq \sum_ {h \in \delta^ {\uparrow} (u)} x _ {h} p \tau f _ {u} \cdot \frac {m _ {\mathbf {e} , u}}{z _ {u} x (\delta^ {\uparrow} (u))} + p \tau \left(1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5}\right) f _ {v} m _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \\ = p \tau f _ {u} m _ {\mathbf {e}, u} + p \tau \left(1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5}\right) f _ {v} m _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \quad (z _ {u} = 1 \text {a s} | \mathcal {a} (s) | = 3) \\ = p \tau \left(f _ {u} m _ {\mathbf {e}, u} + f _ {v} m _ {\mathbf {e}, v}\right) - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5} p \tau f _ {v} m _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \\ \leq p \tau \left(1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) x _ {\mathbf {e}} - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5} p \tau f _ {v} m _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \tag {47} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the final inequality follows from (26). to complete the proof, we lower bound  $m_{\mathbf{e},v}$

using (27) for  $v$  and  $w$ , we can write,

$$
\begin{array}{l} x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (v)\right) + x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (w)\right) = m _ {\mathbf {e}, v} + m _ {\mathbf {g}, v} + m _ {\mathbf {f}, w} + m _ {\mathbf {g}, w} \\ \leq m _ {\mathbf {e}, v} + \frac {(1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta})}{(1 - \epsilon_ {b})} \left(x _ {\mathbf {f}} + x _ {\mathbf {g}}\right) \quad \text {(u s i n g (2 6))} \\ = m _ {\mathbf {e}, v} + \frac {(1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta})}{(1 - \epsilon_ {b})} \left(\sum_ {a \in \mathcal {a} (s)} \frac {x (\delta (a))}{2} - \frac {x (\delta (s))}{2} - x _ {\mathbf {e}}\right) \\ \leq m _ {\mathbf {e}, v} + \frac {(1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta})}{(1 - \epsilon_ {b})} (2 + 3 \epsilon_ {\eta} - x _ {\mathbf {e}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

and using the fact that  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(v)) + x(\delta^{\uparrow}(w))\geq 2 - \epsilon_{f}$  , we get

$$
m _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \geq x _ {\mathbf {e}} - \epsilon_ {f} - 4 \epsilon_ {b} \geq (1 - 1. 2 \epsilon_ {f}) x _ {\mathbf {e}},
$$

where the second inequality follows from (46) and  $\epsilon_b = 21\epsilon_{1/2}$  and  $\epsilon_\eta < \epsilon_{1/2}^2$  and  $\epsilon_f \geq 1/10$ . plugging this back into (47) and using  $f_v \geq 1 - \epsilon_b = 1 - 21\epsilon_{1/2}$  we get

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, u} \right] + \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \right] \leq p \tau x _ {\mathbf {e}} \left(1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{5} (1 - 1. 2 \epsilon_ {f}) (1 - 2 1 \epsilon_ {1 / 2})\right) \leq p \tau x _ {\mathbf {e}} (1 - \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 1}}{6})
$$

as desired. in the last inequality we used  $\epsilon_f \leq 1/10$  and  $\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.0002$ .

case 2:  $|s|\geq 4$  . in this case,  $z_{u} = 2$  . therefore, by eq. (44)

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, u} \right] \leq \sum_ {e \in \delta^ {\uparrow} (u)} x _ {e} p \tau f _ {u} \frac {m _ {\mathbf {e} , u}}{z _ {u} x (\delta^ {\uparrow} (u))} = \frac {1}{2} p \tau f _ {u} m _ {\mathbf {e}, u}.
$$

if  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(v)) < \epsilon_{f}$ , we get the same inequality for  $i_{\mathbf{e},v}$ . then,

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, u} \right] + \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \right] \leq \frac {1}{2} p \tau \left(f _ {u} m _ {\mathbf {e}, u} + f _ {v} m _ {\mathbf {e}, v}\right) \underset {(2 6)} {\leq} \frac {1}{2} p \tau x _ {e} (1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}),
$$

which is clearly sufficient for the lemma statement.

otherwise,  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(v))\geq \epsilon_{f}$  in which case by (45) we get  $\mathbb{e}[i_{\mathbf{e},v}]\leq m_{\mathbf{e},v}p\tau f_v(1 - \epsilon_{1 / 1} / 5)$ . we conclude the lemma similar to the previous case.

# 7.2 increase for bottom edges

the following lemma is the main result of this subsection.

lemma 7.7 (bottom edge increase). if  $\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.0002$ ,  $\epsilon_{\eta} \leq \epsilon_{1/2}^2$ , for any polygon cut  $s \in \mathcal{h}$

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} \right] \leq 0. 9 9 9 9 4 \beta p.
$$

proof. for a set of edges  $d \subseteq \delta(s)$  define the random variable.

$$
\begin{array}{l} i _ {s} (d) := \left(1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) \left(\max  \left\{r (a \cap d) \mathbb {i} \left\{s \text {n o t l e f t h a p p y} \right\}, r (b \cap d) \mathbb {i} \left\{s \text {n o t r i g h t h a p p y} \right\} \right\} \right. \\ + r (c \cap d) \mathbb {i} \{s \text {n o t h a p p y} \}). \tag {48} \\ \end{array}
$$

note that by definition  $i_s(\delta(s)) = i_s$  and for any two disjoint sets  $d_1, d_2$ ,  $i_s(d_1 \cup d_2) \leq i_s(d_1) + i_s(d_2)$ . also, define  $i_s^\uparrow = i_s(\delta^\uparrow(s))$  and  $i_s^\rightarrow = i_s(\delta^\rightarrow(s))$ .

first, we upper bound  $\mathbb{e}\left[i_s^\uparrow\right]$ . let  $f \in \delta^\uparrow(s)$  and suppose that  $f$  with  $\mathfrak{p}(f) = s'$  is a bottom edge. say we have  $f \in a^\uparrow(s)$  ( $f \in b^\uparrow(s)$  is similar). we write,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} (f) \right] = \left(1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) \beta x _ {f} \mathbb {p} \left[ \mathcal {r} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \right] \mathbb {p} \left[ s \text {n o t l e f t h a p p y} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {s ^ {\prime}} \right] \\ \leq 0. 5 6 8 x _ {f} p \beta \leq x _ {f} p \tau \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the inequality we used corollary 5.11 and that

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ s \text {n o t l e f t h a p p y} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {s} \right] = \mathbb {p} \left[ s \text {n o t l e f t h a p p y} \mid \mathcal {e} _ {s} \right]
$$

since  $\mathcal{r}_s$  is a uniformly random subset of  $\mathcal{e}_s$ . if  $f \in c^{\uparrow}(s)$ , we use the trivial guarantee  $\mathbb{e}\left[i_s(f)\right] \leq (1 + \epsilon_\eta)x_f p\beta$ .

on the other hand, if  $f$  is a top edge, then we use the trivial bound

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} (f) \right] \leq \left(1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) \tau p x _ {f}. \tag {49}
$$

therefore,

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} ^ {\uparrow} \right] \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) \tau p x (\delta^ {\uparrow} (s)) + (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) \epsilon_ {\eta} p \beta \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) (0. 5 7 1) \beta p x (\delta^ {\uparrow} (s)) + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} p \beta \tag {50}
$$

since  $x(c)\leq \epsilon_{\eta}$

now, we consider three cases:

case 1:  $\hat{s} = \mathfrak{p}(s)$  is a degree cut. combining (50) and lemma 7.8 below, we get

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} \right] \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) p (0. 5 7 1) \beta (7 / 4 + 6 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} + \epsilon_ {\eta}) + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} p \beta \leq 0. 9 9 9 9 4 \beta p
$$

using  $\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.0002$  and  $\epsilon_{\eta} \leq \epsilon_{1/2}^2$ .

case 2:  $\hat{s} = \mathfrak{p}(s)$  is a polygon cut with ordering  $u_{1},\ldots ,u_{k}$  of  $\mathcal{a}(\hat{s})$ ,  $s = u_{1}$  or  $s = u_{k}$ . then, by lemma 7.9 below,

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} \right] \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) \beta p \left(0. 5 7 1 x \left(\delta^ {\uparrow} (s)\right) + 0. 3 1\right) + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} p \beta \leq 0. 8 9 \beta p
$$

where we used  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(s))\leq 1 + \epsilon_{\eta}$

case 3:  $\hat{s} = \mathfrak{p}(s)$  is a polygon cut with ordering  $u_{1},\ldots ,u_{k}$  of  $\mathcal{a}(\hat{s})$ ,  $s\neq u_1,u_k$  then, by lemma 7.11 below

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} \right] \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) \beta p (0. 5 7 1 x (\delta^ {\uparrow} (s)) + 0. 8 5) + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} p \beta \leq 0. 8 6 \beta p
$$

where we use that  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(s))\leq \epsilon_{\eta}$  since we have a hierarchy. this concludes the proof.

![](images/63e28899269b1196e717fad8fe45731e6b2c23b5ecc3eb31bb3838e89809ec94.jpg)

# 7.2.1 case 1:  $\hat{s}$  is a degree cut

lemma 7.8. let  $s \in \mathcal{h}$  be a polygon cut with parent  $\hat{s}$  which is a degree cut. then

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] \leq \left(1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) p \tau \left(x \left(\delta^ {\rightarrow} (s)\right) - \left(1 / 4 - 6 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}\right)\right).
$$

proof. let  $a, b, c$  be the polygon partition of  $s$ . we will show that for a constant fraction of the edges in  $\delta^{\rightarrow}(s)$ , we can improve over the trivial bound in (49). to this end, consider the cases given by theorem 5.28.

case 1: there is a bad half edge  $e$  in  $\delta^{\rightarrow}(s)$ . since bad edges never decrease, no corresponding increase occurs, so by the trivial bound eq. (49)

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) p \tau \left(x \left(\delta^ {\rightarrow} (s)\right) - \left(1 / 2 - \epsilon_ {1 / 2}\right)\right).
$$

this concludes the proof.

case 2: there is a set of 2-1-1 good edges (w.r.t.,  $s$ )  $d \subseteq \delta^{\rightarrow}(s)$ , such that  $x_{d} \geq 1/2 - \epsilon_{1/2} - \epsilon_{\eta}$ . for any (top) edge  $e \in \mathbf{f} = (s, u)$  such that  $e \in d$ , if  $\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f}, s}$ , then  $s$  is happy, that is  $a_{t} = b_{t} = 1, c_{t} = 0$  by remark 5.20.

therefore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} (d) \right] \leq \sum_ {e \in d: e \in \mathbf {f} = (s, u)} \frac {1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}}{2} \tau x _ {e} \mathbb {p} \left[ s \text {n o t h a p p y} \left| \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u} \right] \mathbb {p} \left[ \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, u} \right. \right. \\ \leq \frac {1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}}{2} p \tau x (d). \\ \end{array}
$$

using the trivial inequality eq. (49) for edges in  $\delta^{\rightarrow}(s)\setminus d$  we get

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] \leq \left(1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) p \tau \left(\frac {x (d)}{2} + x (\delta^ {\rightarrow} (s)) - x (d)\right) \leq \left(1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) p \tau \left(x \left(\delta^ {\rightarrow} (s)\right) - \left(1 / 4 - \epsilon_ {1 / 2}\right)\right)
$$

as desired. in the last inequality we used  $x(d) \geq 1/2 - \epsilon_{1/2} - \epsilon_{\eta}$ .

case 3: cases 1 and 2 do not hold. therefore, by theorem 5.28 there are at least two 2-2-2 good top half edge bundles. in this case,  $s$  has chosen a fixed pair of 2-2-2 good edges  $\mathbf{e} = (s,v)$ ,  $\mathbf{f} = (s,w)$  in  $\delta^{\rightarrow}(s)$  (as defined in the reduction events) such that  $x_{\mathbf{e}(b)}, x_{\mathbf{f}(a)} \leq \epsilon_{1/2}$  and  $\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{e},s} = \mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},s}$  with probability 1. (recall that  $\mathbf{e}(a) = \mathbf{e} \cap a$ .) let  $d = \mathbf{e}(a) \cup \mathbf{f}(b)$ . in this case,  $\mathbf{e}$  and  $\mathbf{f}$  are reduced simultaneously by  $\tau$  when they are 2-2-2 happy (w.r.t.,  $s$ ), i.e., when  $\mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{e},s} = \mathcal{r}_{\mathbf{f},s} = 1$ . in such a case we have  $\delta(s)_t = \delta(v)_t = \delta(w)_t = 2$ . therefore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} (d) \right] \leq \left(1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) \mathbb {e} \left[ \max  \{r (a \cap d), r (b \cap d) \} \right] \\ \leq \left(1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) \frac {\tau}{2} \max  \left\{x _ {\mathbf {e} (a)}, x _ {\mathbf {f} (b)} \right\} \left(\mathbb {p} \left[ \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {e}, s} \wedge \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, s} \right] + \mathbb {p} \left[ \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {e}, v} \right] + \mathbb {p} \left[ \mathcal {r} _ {\mathbf {f}, w} \right]\right) \\ \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) \tau \frac {3 p}{2} x (d) \left(\frac {1}{2} + 3 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}\right) = (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) \tau p x (d) \left(\frac {3}{4} + 4. 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used that  $1/2 - 2\epsilon_{1/2} - x(c) \leq x_{\mathbf{e}(a)}, x_{\mathbf{f}(b)} \leq 1/2 + \epsilon_{1/2}$  and that  $x(c) \leq \epsilon_{\eta}$ . using the trivial inequality eq. (49) for edges in  $\delta^{\rightarrow}(s) \setminus d$  we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) p \tau (x (d) (3 / 4 + 4. 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}) + x (\delta^ {\rightarrow} (s)) - x (d)) \\ \leq \left(1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) p \tau \left(x \left(\delta^ {\rightarrow} (s)\right) - \left(1 / 4 - 6 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}\right)\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $x(d) \geq 1 - 4\epsilon_{1/2} - \epsilon_{\eta}$ .

□

# 7.2.2 case 2:  $s$  and its parent  $\hat{s}$  are both polygon cuts

in this subsection we prove two lemmas: lemma 7.9, which bounds  $\mathbb{e}[i_s^{\rightarrow}]$  when  $s$  is the leftmost or rightmost atom of  $\hat{s}$ , and lemma 7.11, which bounds this quantity when  $s$  is not leftmost or rightmost.

lemma 7.9. let  $s \in \mathcal{h}$  be a polygon cut with  $\mathfrak{p}(s) = \hat{s}$  also a polygon cut. let  $u_1, \ldots, u_k$  be the ordering of cuts in  $\mathcal{a}(\hat{s})$  (as defined in definition 4.31). if  $\epsilon_m \leq 0.001$ ,  $\epsilon_\eta \leq \epsilon_m^2$ ,  $s = u_1$  or  $s = u_k$ , then

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] \leq 0. 3 1 \beta p.
$$

proof. let  $s$  be the leftmost atom of  $\hat{s}$  and let  $a, b, c$  be the polygon partition of  $\delta(s)$ . first, note

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] \leq \left(1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}\right)\left(\mathbb {e} \left[ \max  \left(r (a ^ {\rightarrow}), r (b ^ {\rightarrow})\right) \cdot \mathbb {i} \{s \text {n o t h a p p y} \} \right] + \mathbb {e} \left[ r (c ^ {\rightarrow}) \mathbb {i} \{s \text {n o t h a p p y} \} \right]\right). \tag {51}
$$

where recall that  $a\to = a\cap \delta^{\to}(s)$  . wlog assume  $x(a\rightarrow)\geq x(b\rightarrow)$  . then,

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \max  \{r (a ^ {\rightarrow}), r (b ^ {\rightarrow}) \} \mathbb {i} \left\{s \text {n o t h a p p y} \right\}\right] = \beta p x (a ^ {\rightarrow}) \cdot \mathbb {p} \left[ s \text {n o t h a p p y} | \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} \right]
$$

by lemma 7.10 we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right) \cdot \mathbb {p} [ s \text {n o t h a p p y} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} ] \leq x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right)\left(1 - \left(\left(1 - x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right)\right) ^ {2} + \left(x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right)\right) ^ {2} - 2 \epsilon_ {m} - 1 7 \epsilon_ {\eta}\right)\right) \\ \leq \left(2 x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right) ^ {2} - 2 x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right) ^ {3} + 2 \epsilon_ {m} x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right) + 1 7 \epsilon_ {\eta} x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right)\right) \\ \leq (8 / 2 7 + 2 \epsilon_ {m} + 1 7 \epsilon_ {\eta}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the final inequality we used that the function  $x \mapsto x^2(1 - x)$  is maximized at  $x = 2/3$ , and using  $\epsilon_m \leq 0.001, \epsilon_\eta < \epsilon_m^2$ .

plugging this back into (51), and using  $x(c) \leq \epsilon_{\eta}$ , we get

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) \beta p \left(\frac {8}{2 7} + 2 \epsilon_ {m} + 1 8 \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) \leq 0. 3 1 \beta p,
$$

where the last inequality follows since  $\epsilon_{m} \leq 0.001$  and  $\epsilon_{\eta} < \epsilon_{m}^{2}$ .

lemma 7.10. let  $s \in \mathcal{h}$  be a polygon cut with  $\mathfrak{p}(s) = \hat{s}$  also a polygon cut. let  $u_1, \ldots, u_k$  be the ordering of cuts in  $\mathcal{a}(\hat{s})$ . if  $s = u_1$ , (or  $s = u_k$ ) then

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ s h a p p y | \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} \right] \geq (1 - x (a ^ {\rightarrow})) ^ {2} + (x (a ^ {\rightarrow})) ^ {2} - 2 \epsilon_ {m} - 1 7 \epsilon_ {\eta}.
$$

proof. let  $a, b, c, \hat{a}, \hat{b}, \hat{c}$  be the polygon partition of  $s, \hat{s}$  respectively. observe that since  $s = u_{1}$ , we have  $\hat{a} = e(u_{1}, \overline{\hat{s}}) = a^{\uparrow} \cup b^{\uparrow} \cup c^{\uparrow}$  and  $\hat{b}, \hat{c} \cap (a \cup b \cup c) = \emptyset$ . conditioned on  $\mathcal{r}_{\hat{s}}, \hat{s}$  is a tree, and marginals of all edges in  $\hat{a}$  is changed by a total variation distance at most  $\epsilon_{m}' := \epsilon_{m} + 2\epsilon_{\eta}$  from  $x$  (see corollary 5.9) and they are independent of edges inside  $\hat{s}$ . the tree conditioning increases marginals inside by at most  $\epsilon_{\eta} / 2$ . since after the changes just described

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ c _ {t} \right] \leq x _ {c} + \epsilon_ {\eta} + \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime} \leq 4 \epsilon_ {\eta} + \epsilon_ {m},
$$

it follows that  $\mathbb{p}\left[c_t = 0|\mathcal{r}_{\hat{s}}\right] \geq 1 - 4\epsilon_\eta - \epsilon_m$ . so,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ s \text {h a p p y} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} \right] \geq \left(1 - 4 \epsilon_ {\eta} - \epsilon_ {m}\right) \mathbb {p} \left[ a _ {t} = b _ {t} = 1 \mid c _ {t} = 0, \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} \right]. \tag {52}
$$

let  $\nu$  be the conditional measure  $c_t = 0$ ,  $\mathcal{r}_{\hat{s}}$ . we see that

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} = b _ {t} = 1 \right] = \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\uparrow} = 1, b _ {t} ^ {\uparrow} = 0, a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 0, b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1 \right] + \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\uparrow} = 0, b _ {t} ^ {\uparrow} = 1, a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1, b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 0 \right]
$$

so using independence of  $(\delta^{\uparrow}(s))_t$  and  $(\delta^{\rightarrow}(s))_t$ .

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\uparrow} = 1, b _ {t} ^ {\uparrow} = 0 \right] \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 0, b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1 \right] + \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\uparrow} = 0, b _ {t} ^ {\uparrow} = 1 \right] \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1, b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 0 \right] \\ \geq (x (a ^ {\uparrow}) - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}) \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} [ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 0, b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1 ] + (x (b ^ {\uparrow}) - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}) \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} [ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1, b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 0 ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

in the final inequality, we used the fact that conditioned on  $\mathcal{r}_{\hat{\mathcal{s}}}$ ,  $\hat{a} = (a^{\uparrow} \cup b^{\uparrow} \cup c^{\uparrow})_{t} = 1$  and marginals in  $a^{\uparrow}$  and  $b^{\uparrow}$  are approximately preserved. now, we lower bound  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[a_t^{\rightarrow} = 1, b_t^{\rightarrow} = 0]$ . let  $\epsilon_a, \epsilon_b$  be such that

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] = \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1, b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 0 \right] + \epsilon_ {a}, \quad \mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] = \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 0, b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1 \right] + \epsilon_ {b}
$$

first notice that  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}\left[a_t^{\rightarrow} + b_t^{\rightarrow}\geq 1\right] = 1,$  and so  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}\left[a_t^{\rightarrow} + b_t^{\rightarrow}\geq 2\right]\leq \mathbb{e}_{\nu}\left[a_t^{\rightarrow} + b_t^{\rightarrow}\right] - 1.$  so,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \epsilon_ {a} + \epsilon_ {b} = \mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} + b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] - \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} + b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1 \right] = \mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} + b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] - (1 - \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} + b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \geq 2 \right]) \\ \leq 2 \left(\mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} + b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] - 1\right) \leq 5 \epsilon_ {\eta}. \\ \end{array}
$$

to see the last inequality, first, by definition 4.31,  $x(\delta^{\uparrow}(s)) \geq 1 - \epsilon_{\eta}$ . since  $x(\delta(s)) \leq 2 + \epsilon_{\eta}$ , we get that  $x(\delta^{\rightarrow}(s)) \leq 1 + 2\epsilon_{\eta}$ . therefore,

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} + b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] \leq \mathbb {e} \left[ \delta^ {\rightarrow} (s) \mid \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} \right] \leq x (\delta^ {\rightarrow} (s)) + \epsilon_ {\eta} / 2 \leq 1 + 2. 5 \epsilon_ {\eta}.
$$

therefore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} = b _ {t} = 1 \right] \geq \left(x \left(a ^ {\uparrow}\right) - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}\right)\left(\mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] - \epsilon_ {b}\right) + \left(x \left(b ^ {\uparrow}\right) - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}\right)\left(\mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] - \epsilon_ {a}\right) \\ \geq \left(x \left(a ^ {\uparrow}\right) - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}\right)\left(x \left(b ^ {\rightarrow}\right) - 5 \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) + \left(x \left(b ^ {\uparrow}\right) - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}\right)\left(x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right) - 5 \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality uses that the tree conditioning and  $c_t^{\rightarrow} = 0$  can only increase the margins of edges in  $a^{\rightarrow}$  and  $b^{\rightarrow}$ . simplify the above using  $x(a^{\uparrow}) + x(a^{\rightarrow}) \geq 1 - \epsilon_{\eta}$ , and similarly for  $b$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} = b _ {t} = 1 \right] \\ \geq (1 - x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right) - \epsilon_ {\eta} - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}) \left(x \left(b ^ {\rightarrow}\right) - 5 \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) + (1 - x \left(b ^ {\rightarrow}\right) - \epsilon_ {\eta} - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}) \left(x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right) - 5 \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

and since  $x(a^{\rightarrow}) + x(b^{\rightarrow})\geq 1 - 2\epsilon_{\eta}$  (because  $x(a^{\uparrow}) + x(b^{\uparrow})\leq 1 + \epsilon_{\eta}$  and  $x_{c}\leq \epsilon_{\eta}$ ), this is

$$
\begin{array}{l} \geq (1 - x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right) - \epsilon_ {\eta} - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}) (1 - x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right) - 7 \epsilon_ {\eta}) + (x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right) - 3 \epsilon_ {\eta} - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}) (x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right) - 5 \epsilon_ {\eta}) \\ \geq (1 - x (a ^ {\rightarrow})) ^ {2} + (x (a ^ {\rightarrow})) ^ {2} - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime} - 8 \epsilon_ {\eta}. \\ \end{array}
$$

plugging this into eq. (52), we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left[ a _ {t} = b _ {t} = 1, c _ {t} = 0 \mid \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} \right] \geq (1 - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}) \mathbb {p} \left[ a _ {t} = b _ {t} = 1 \mid c _ {t} = 0, \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} \right] \\ \geq \left(1 - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta} - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime}\right)\left(\left(1 - x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right)\right) ^ {2} + \left(x \left(a ^ {\rightarrow}\right)\right) ^ {2} - \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime} - 8 \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) \\ \geq \left(1 - x (a ^ {\rightarrow})\right) ^ {2} + \left(x (a ^ {\rightarrow})\right) ^ {2} - 2 \epsilon_ {m} ^ {\prime} - 1 0 \epsilon_ {\eta}, \\ \end{array}
$$

which noting  $\epsilon_m^\prime = \epsilon_m + 2\epsilon_\eta$  completes the proof of the lemma.

![](images/e0ea1e587b859e3c49c5635eb9cd436808cff1f77503fdf733c32b4773ff07dc.jpg)

lemma 7.11. let  $s \in \mathcal{h}$  be a polygon cut with  $\mathsf{p}(s) = \hat{s}$  also a polygon cut with  $u_1, \ldots, u_k$  be the ordering of cuts in  $\mathcal{a}(\hat{s})$ . if  $s \neq u_1, u_k$ , then

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] \leq 0. 8 5 \beta p.
$$

proof. let  $s = u_{i}$  for some  $2 \leq i \leq k - 1$ . let  $a, b, c$  be the polygon partitioning of  $\delta(u_{i})$  and  $\hat{a}, \hat{b}, \hat{c}$  be the polygon partition of  $\hat{s}$ . since  $u_{i}$  is in the hierarchy  $a^{\uparrow} \cup b^{\uparrow} \cup c^{\uparrow} \subseteq \hat{c}$ . so, conditioned on  $\mathcal{r}_{\hat{s}}$ ,  $a_{t}^{\uparrow} = b_{t}^{\uparrow} = c_{t}^{\uparrow} = 0$ .

once again, let  $\nu$  be the conditional measure  $c_t = 0$ ,  $\mathcal{r}_{\hat{s}}$ . similar to the previous case, we will lower-bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left[ s \text {h a p p y} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} \right] \geq (1 - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}) \mathbb {p} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1, b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1, \mid c _ {t} = 0, \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} \right] \\ = (1 - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}) \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} [ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1 | a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} + b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 2 ] \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} [ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} + b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 2 ] \tag {53} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\mathbb{e}\left[c_t^{\rightarrow}|\mathcal{r}_{\hat{s}}\right] \leq 2\epsilon_{\eta}$  in the first inequality. so, it remains to lower-bound each of the two terms in the rhs.

we start with the first one. since  $x(a) \in [1 - \epsilon_{\eta}, 1 + \epsilon_{\eta}]$  and  $x(a^{\uparrow}) \leq \epsilon_{\eta}$  we have

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] \in \left[ 1 - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}, 1 + 3 \epsilon_ {\eta} \right].
$$

the same bounds hold for  $\mathbb{e}_{\nu}[x(b^{\rightarrow})]$ .

therefore,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \geq 1 \right], \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \geq 1 \right] \geq 1 - e ^ {- 1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}} \tag {lemma 2.22}
$$

$$
\left. \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \leq 1 \right], \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} \leq 1 \right] \geq 0. 4 9 5 \right. \tag {markov}
$$

therefore, by corollary 5.5 (with  $\epsilon = 0.495(1 - e^{-1 + 2\epsilon_{\eta}}) \geq 0.31$ ) we have

$$
\mathrm {i p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 1 \mid a _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} + b _ {t} ^ {\rightarrow} = 2 \right] \geq 0. 1 5 5.
$$

by corollary 2.24,  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}\left[e(u_{i - 1},u_i)_t = 1\right]\geq 1 - 4\epsilon_{\eta}$ . similarly,  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}\left[e(u_i,u_{i + 1})_t = 1\right]\geq 1 - 4\epsilon_{\eta}$ . and,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ \delta^ {\rightarrow} (u _ {i}) _ {t} - e (u _ {i - 1}, u _ {i}) _ {t} - e (u _ {i}, u _ {i + 1}) _ {t} = 0 \right] \geq 1 - 4 \epsilon_ {\eta}
$$

so, by a union bound all of these events happen simultaneously and we get  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[\delta^{\rightarrow}(u_i)_t = 2] \geq 1 - 12\epsilon_{\eta}$ . therefore,

$$
\mathrm {i p} _ {\nu} \left[ (a ^ {\rightarrow}) _ {t} = (b ^ {\rightarrow}) _ {t} = 1 \right] \geq 0. 1 5 5 \left(1 - 1 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) \geq 0. 1 5 3.
$$

plugging this back into (53), we get

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ s \text {h a p p y} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} \right] \geq 0. 1 5 3 (1 - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}) \geq 0. 1 5 2.
$$

plugging this in (51) we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ i _ {s} ^ {\rightarrow} \right] \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) \beta p \mathbb {p} \left[ s \text {n o t h a p p y} \mid \mathcal {r} _ {\hat {s}} \right] (\max  \{x (a ^ {\rightarrow}), x (b ^ {\rightarrow}) \} + x (c ^ {\rightarrow})) \\ \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta}) \beta p (1 - 0. 1 5 2) (1 + \epsilon_ {\eta} + \epsilon_ {\eta}) \leq 0. 8 5 \beta p \\ \end{array}
$$

as desired.

□

# a proofs from section 5

in all of the following lemmas, we assume that  $\epsilon_{\eta} \leq \epsilon_{1/2}^2$  and  $12\epsilon_{1/1} \leq \epsilon_{1/2}$ .

![](images/fb56271ff1b3ab6b72a68c0724e07b874b2be5b9ba65f2305090167d431d7902.jpg)  
figure 13: setting of lemma 5.21

lemma 5.21. let  $\mathbf{e} = (\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})$  be a top edge bundle such that  $x_{\mathbf{e}}\leq 1 / 2 - \epsilon_{1 / 2}$ . if  $\epsilon_{1 / 2}\leq 0.001$  then,  $\mathbf{e}$  is 2-1-1 happy with probability at least  $0.005\epsilon_{1 / 2}^{2}$ .

proof. let  $a, b, c$  be the degree partitioning of  $\delta(u)$ . let  $v := \delta(v)_{-\mathbf{e}}$  (see fig. 13). condition  $u, v$  be trees,  $\mathbf{e}$  and  $c$  to 0, let  $\nu$  be the resulting measure. this happens with probability at least 0.5 and increases marginals in  $a_{-\mathbf{e}}, b_{-\mathbf{e}}, v$  by at most  $x_{\mathbf{e}} + 2\epsilon_{1/1} + \epsilon_{\eta} \leq x_{\mathbf{e}} + 2.1\epsilon_{1/1}$  and by tree conditioning decreases marginals by at most  $2\epsilon_{\eta}$ . after conditioning, we have

$\mathbb{e}_{\nu}[a_t] \in x(a) - x_{\mathbf{e}(a)} + [-2\epsilon_{\eta}, x_{\mathbf{e}} + 2.1\epsilon_{1/1}] \subset [0.5, 1.5]$ , similarly  $\mathbb{e}_{\nu}[b_t] \subset [0.5, 1.5]$ .  
$\mathbb{e}_{\nu}\left[v_t\right]\in x(\delta (v)) - x_{\mathbf{e}} + \left[-2\epsilon_{\eta},x_{\mathbf{e}} + 2.1\epsilon_{1 / 1}\right]\subset [1.5,2.01]$  
$\mathbb{e}_{\nu}\left[b_t + v_t\right] \in x(b) + x(\delta(v)) - x_{\mathbf{e}} - x_{\mathbf{e}(b)} + \left[-2\epsilon_{\eta}, x_{\mathbf{e}} + 2.1\epsilon_{1/1}\right] \subset [2 + 1.8\epsilon_{1/2}, 3.01]$ ,  
$\mathbb{e}_{\nu}\left[a_t + b_t\right] \in x(a) + x(b) - x_{e(a)} - x_{\mathbf{e}(b)} + \left[-2\epsilon_\eta, x_{\mathbf{e}} + 2.1\epsilon_{1/1}\right] \subset [1.5, 2.01]$ ,  
$\mathbb{e}_{\nu}\left[a_t + b_t + v_t\right] \in x(a) + x(b) + x(\delta(v)) - x_{\mathbf{e}} - x_{e(a)} - x_{\mathbf{e}(b)} + [-2\epsilon_{\eta}, x_{\mathbf{e}} + 2.1\epsilon_{1/1}]$  
$\subset [3 + 1.75\epsilon_{1 / 2},4.01]$

where we used  $\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.001$  and  $12\epsilon_{1/1} < \epsilon_{1/2}$  and  $x_{e(a)}, x_{\mathbf{e}(b)}, x_{e(a)} + x_{\mathbf{e}(b)} \leq x_{\mathbf{e}} \leq 1/2 - \epsilon_{1/2}$ . it immediately follows from proposition 5.1 that  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}\left[a_t = b_t = 1, v_t = 2\right]$  is at least a constant. in the rest of the proof, we do a more refined analysis. using  $a_t + b_t \geq 1, v_t \geq 1$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + b _ {t} + v _ {t} = 4 \right] \geq \left(1. 7 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}\right) e ^ {- 1. 7 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}} \geq 1. 7 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}, \tag {lemma 2.21}
$$

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + b _ {t} \geq 2 \right], \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ v _ {t} \geq 2 \right] \geq 0. 3 9, \tag {lemma 2.22}
$$

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + b _ {t} \leq 2 \right], \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ v _ {t} \leq 2 \right] \geq 0. 5, \quad (\text {m a r k o v}, a _ {t} + b _ {t} \geq 1, v _ {t} \geq 1 \text {u n d e r} \nu)
$$

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} \leq 1 \right] \geq 0. 2 5, \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ b _ {t} + v _ {t} \leq 3 \right] \geq 0. 3 3. \quad (\text {m a r k o v ' s i n e q u a l i t y a n d} v _ {t} \geq 1 \text {u n d e r} \nu)
$$

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} \geq 1 \right] \geq 0. 3 9, \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ b _ {t} + v _ {t} \geq 3 \right] \geq 1. 7 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}, \tag {lemma 2.22}
$$

it follows by corollary 5.5 (with  $\epsilon = 0.195, p_m \geq 1 - 2\epsilon \geq 0.6$ ) that

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ v _ {t} = 2 \mid a _ {t} + b _ {t} + v _ {t} = 4 \right] \geq 0. 1 3.
$$

note that since  $v_{t} \geq 1, a_{t} + b_{t} \geq 1$  with probability 1, we apply corollary 5.5 to  $v_{t} - 1, a_{t} + b_{t} - 1$ .

furthermore, by lemma 5.4,  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[a_t \geq 1|a_t + b_t + v_t = 4] \geq 0.128$ ,  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[a_t \leq 1|a_t + b_t + v_t = 4] \geq 0.43\epsilon_{1/2}$ . the same holds for  $b_t$ . therefore, by corollary 5.5 (with  $\epsilon = 0.055\epsilon_{1/2}$ ), using that  $\epsilon_{1/2} < 0.001$ ,

$$
\mathrm {i p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} = 1 \mid a _ {t} + b _ {t} = 2, v _ {t} = 2 \right] \geq 0. 0 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}.
$$

putting these together we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} [ e 2 - 1 - 1 \text {h a p p y} ] \geq 0. 5 \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} [ a _ {t} = b _ {t} = 1, v _ {t} = 2 ] \\ = 0. 5 \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + b _ {t} + v _ {t} = 4 \right] \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ v _ {t} = 2 \mid a _ {t} + b _ {t} + v _ {t} = 4 \right] \\ \cdot \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} [ a _ {t} = 1 | v _ {t} = 2, a _ {t} + b _ {t} = 2 ] \\ \geq 0. 5 (1. 7 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}) (0. 1 3) (0. 0 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}) \geq 0. 0 0 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

as desired.

![](images/72b5b9af1377e14e0d454a0f4e2e33778dd8b3717ac6b88902c47fe190032938.jpg)

lemma 5.22. let  $\mathbf{e} = (\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})$  be a top edge bundle such that  $x_{\mathbf{e}}\geq 1 / 2 + \epsilon_{1 / 2}$ . if  $\epsilon_{1 / 2}\leq 0.001$ , then,  $\mathbf{e}$  is 2-1-1 happy with respect to  $u$  with probability at least  $0.006\epsilon_{1 / 2}^{2}$ .

proof. let  $a, b, c$  be the degree partitioning of the edges in  $\delta(u)$ ,  $v = \delta_{-\mathbf{e}}(v)$ . condition  $u, v$  be trees,  $c_t = 0$  and  $u \cup v$  to be a tree (in order). this happens with probability at least  $\frac{1}{2} + \epsilon_{1/2} - 3\epsilon_{\eta} - 2\epsilon_{1/1} \geq 0.5$ . let  $\nu$  be the resulting measure restricted to edges in  $a, b, v$ . note that  $\nu$  on edges in  $a, b, v$  is sr. this is because  $\nu$  is a product of two strongly rayleigh distributions on the following two disjoint sets of edges (i) the edges between  $u, v$  and (ii) the edges in  $a_{-\mathbf{e}}, b_{-\mathbf{e}}, v$ .

furthermore, observe that under  $\nu$ , every set of edges in  $a_{-\mathbf{e}}, b_{-\mathbf{e}}, v$  increases by at most  $2\epsilon_{1/1} + \epsilon_{\eta} < 0.2\epsilon_{1/2}$  (using  $12\epsilon_{1/1} \leq \epsilon_{1/2}$ ), and decreases by at most  $1 - x_{\mathbf{e}} + 2\epsilon_{\eta}$ . therefore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} \right] \in x (a) + \left[ - \left(1 - x _ {\mathbf {e}}\right) - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}, 1 - x _ {\mathbf {e}} + 0. 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} \right] \subset [ 0. 5, 1. 5 ], \text {s i m i l a r l y ,} \mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ b _ {t} \right] \in [ 0. 5, 1. 5 ] \\ \mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ v _ {t} \right] \in x (\delta (v)) - x _ {\mathbf {e}} + \left[ - \left(1 - x _ {\mathbf {e}}\right) - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}, 0. 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} \right] \subset [ 0. 9 9 5, 1. 5 ]. \\ \mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + b _ {t} \right] \in x (a) + x (b) + 1 - x _ {e (a)} - x _ {\mathbf {e} (b)} + \left[ - \left(1 - x _ {\mathbf {e}}\right) - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}, 0. 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} \right] \subset [ 1. 9 9 5, 2. 5 ], \\ \mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ b _ {t} + v _ {t} \right] \in x (b) + x (\delta (v)) - x _ {\mathbf {e}} + \left[ - \left(1 - x _ {\mathbf {e}}\right) - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}, 1 - x _ {\mathbf {e}} + 0. 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} \right] \subset [ 1. 9 9, 3 - 1. 7 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} ]. \\ \mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + b _ {t} + v _ {t} \right] \in x (a) + x (b) + x (\delta (v)) + 1 - x _ {\mathbf {e}} - x _ {e (a)} - x _ {\mathbf {e} (b)} + \left[ - \left(1 - x _ {\mathbf {e}}\right) - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}, 0. 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} \right] \\ \subset [ 2. 9 9, 4 - 1. 7 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the upper bound on  $\mathbb{e}_{\nu}[a_t], \mathbb{e}_{\nu}[b_t], \mathbb{e}_{\nu}[b_t + v_t]$  we used that the marginals of edges in the bundle  $\mathbf{e}$  can only increase by  $1 - x_{\mathbf{e}}$  (in total) when conditioning  $u \cup v$  to be a tree. so,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + b _ {t} + v _ {t} = 3 \right] \geq \epsilon_ {1 / 2},
$$

(by theorem 2.15)

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + b _ {t} \geq 2 \right] \geq 0. 6 3, \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ v _ {t} \geq 1 \right] \geq 0. 6 3
$$

(lemma 2.22,  $a_t + b_t \geq 1$ )

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + b _ {t} \leq 2 \right] \geq 0. 2 5, \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ v _ {t} \leq 1 \right] \geq 0. 2 5,
$$

(markov inequality,  $a_t + b_t \geq 1$ )

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} \geq 1 \right] \geq 0. 3 9, \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ b _ {t} + v _ {t} \geq 2 \right] \geq 0. 5 9
$$

(lemma 2.22)

$\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[a_t \leq 1] \geq 0.25, \mathbb{p}_{\nu}[b_t + v_t \leq 2] \geq 1.75 \epsilon_{1/2},$  (markov, in worst case  $\mathbb{p}[b_t + v_t < 2] = 0$ )

it follows by corollary 5.5 (with  $\epsilon = 0.157$ ,  $p_m = 0.68$ ) that

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + b _ {t} = 2 \mid a _ {t} + b _ {t} + v _ {t} = 3 \right] \geq 0. 1 2.
$$

note that since  $a_{t} + b_{t}\geq 1$  with probability 1, we apply corollary 5.5 to  $a_{t} + b_{t} - 1,v_{t}$

furthermore, by lemma 5.4,  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}\left[a_t \geq 1 \mid a_t + b_t + v_t = 3\right] \geq 0.68\epsilon_{1/2}$  and  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}\left[a_t \leq 1 \mid a_t + b_t + v_t = 3\right] \geq 0.147$ . by symmetry, the same holds for  $b_t$ . therefore, by corollary 5.5,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} = 1 \mid a _ {t} + b _ {t} = 2, v _ {t} = 1 \right] \geq 0. 0 9 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}.
$$

where we used  $\epsilon_{1 / 2} < 0.001$ .

finally,

$$
\mathbb {p} [ \mathbf {e} 2 - 1 - 1 \text {h a p p y} ] \geq (0. 0 9 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}) 0. 1 2 (\epsilon_ {1 / 2}) 0. 5 \geq 0. 0 0 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} ^ {2},
$$

as desired.

![](images/f7c6b50001e30810be3048a1fcc764fdc6a6b66de87c3618516e5e5f66e4943e.jpg)

![](images/0971bc299c9c53f99c8857f575c97f4ee77abdd78f759167a3d17d3b781c6041.jpg)  
figure 14: setting of lemma a.1

lemma a.1. for a good half top edge bundle  $\mathbf{e} = (\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})$ , let  $a,b,c$  be the degree partitioning of  $\delta (u)$  and let  $v = \delta (v)_{-\mathbf{e}}$  (see fig. 14). if  $\epsilon_{1 / 2}\leq 0.001$ ,  $x_{\mathbf{e}(b)}\leq \epsilon_{1 / 2}$ , and  $\mathbb{p}\left[(a_{-\mathbf{e}})_t + v_t\leq 1\right]\geq 5\epsilon_{1 / 2}$  then  $\mathbf{e}$  is 2-1-1 good,

$$
\mathbb {p} [ \textbf {e} 2 - 1 - 1 h a p p y w. r. t. u ] \geq 0. 0 0 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} ^ {2}
$$

proof. the proof is similar to lemma 5.22. we condition  $u, v$  to be trees,  $c_t = 0$ ,  $u \cup v$  to be a tree. let  $\nu$  be the resulting sr measure on edges in  $a, b, v$ . the main difference is since  $x_{\mathbf{e}} \geq 1/2 + \epsilon_{1/2}$  we use the lemma's assumptions to lower bound  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[a_t + b_t + v_t = 3]$ ,  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[a_t + v_t \leq 2]$ ,  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[b_t + v_t \leq 2]$ .

first, since  $\mathbf{e}$  is 2-2 good, by lemma 5.15 and negative association,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ (\delta (u) _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} + v _ {t} \leq 2 \right] \geq \mathbb {p} \left[ (\delta (u) _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} + v _ {t} \leq 2 \right] - \mathbb {p} \left[ c _ {t} = 0 \right] \geq 0. 4 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} - 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 1} - \epsilon_ {\eta} \geq 0. 2 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2},
$$

where we used  $\epsilon_{1/1} \leq \epsilon_{1/2} / 12$ . letting  $p_i = \mathbb{p}\left[(\delta(u)_{-\mathbf{e}})_t + v_t = i\right]$ , we therefore have  $p_{\leq 2} \geq 0.22\epsilon_{1/2}$ . in addition, by lemma 2.21,  $p_3 \geq 1/4$ . if  $p_2 < 0.2\epsilon_{1/2}$ , then from  $p_2 / p_3 \leq 0.8\epsilon_{1/2}$ , we could use log-concavity to derive a contradiction to  $p_{\leq 2} \geq 0.22\epsilon_{1/2}$  (analogously to what's done in the proof of lemma 2.18). therefore, we must have

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + b _ {t} + v _ {t} = 3 \right] = \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ (\delta (u) _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} + v _ {t} = 2 \right] \geq 0. 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}.
$$

next, notice since  $\mathbb{p}[u,v,u\cup v$  trees,  $c_t = 0]\geq 0.49,$  by the lemma's assumption,  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[\mathbf{e}(b)]\leq$ $2.01\epsilon_{1 / 2}$ . therefore,

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ b _ {t} + v _ {t} \right] \leq x (v) + x (b) + 1. 0 1 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} + 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 1} + \epsilon_ {\eta} \leq 2. 5 1.
$$

so, by markov,  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}\left[b_t + v_t \leq 2\right] \geq 0.15$ . finally, by negative association,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ a _ {t} + v _ {t} \leq 2 \right] \geq \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ \left(a _ {- \mathbf {e}}\right) _ {t} + v _ {t} \leq 1 \right] \geq \mathbb {p} \left[ \left(a _ {- \mathbf {e}}\right) _ {t} + v _ {t} \leq 1 \right] - \mathbb {p} \left[ c _ {t} = 0 \right] \geq 4. 8 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}
$$

where we used the lemma's assumption.

now, following the same line of arguments as in lemma 5.22, we have  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[a_t + b_t = 2|a_t + b_t + v_t = 3] \geq 0.12$ . also,  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[a_t \geq 1|a - t + b_t + v_t = 3] \geq 3.02$ , which implies  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[a_t = 1|a_t + b_t = 2, v_t = 1] \geq 0.42\epsilon$ . this implies

$$
\mathbb {p} [ \mathrm {e} 2 - 1 - 1 \text {h a p p y} ] \geq (0. 4 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}) 0. 1 2 (0. 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}) 0. 4 9 8 \geq 0. 0 0 5 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} ^ {2}
$$

as desired.

lemma 5.23. let  $\mathbf{e} = (\mathbf{v},\mathbf{u})$  and  $\mathbf{f} = (\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})$  be good half top edge bundles and let  $a,b,c$  be the degree partitioning of  $\delta (v)$  such that  $x_{\mathbf{e}(b)},x_{\mathbf{f}(b)}\leq \epsilon_{1 / 2}$ . then, one of  $\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}$  is 2-1-1 happy with probability at least  $0.005\epsilon_{1 / 2}^2$ .

proof. let  $u = \delta(u)_{-\mathbf{e}}$ . by lemma 2.27, we can assume, without loss of generality, that

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ u _ {t} | \mathbf {f} \notin t, u, v, w \text {t r e e} \right] \leq x \left(u _ {t}\right) + 0. 4 0 5 + 3 \epsilon_ {\eta}. \tag {54}
$$

on the other hand,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ (a _ {- \mathbf {e} - \mathbf {f}}) _ {t} \right] \geq \mathbb {e} \left[ (a _ {- \mathbf {e} - \mathbf {f}}) _ {t} | \mathbf {f} \notin t, u, v, w \text {t r e e} \right] \mathbb {p} [ \mathbf {f} \notin t, u, v, w, \text {t r e e} ] \\ \geq \mathbb {e} \left[ (a _ {- \mathbf {e} - \mathbf {f}}) _ {t} | \mathbf {f} \notin t, u, v, w \text {t r e e} \right] 0. 4 9 \\ \end{array}
$$

so,

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \left(a _ {- \mathbf {e} - \mathbf {f}}\right) _ {t} \mid \mathbf {f} \notin t, u, v, w, \text {t r e e} \right] \leq \frac {1}{0 . 4 9} x \left(a _ {- \mathbf {e} - \mathbf {f}}\right) \leq \frac {1}{0 . 4 9} \left(4 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} + \epsilon_ {\eta}\right) \leq 8. 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}. \tag {55}
$$

combining (54) and (55), we get  $\mathbb{e}\left[u_t + (a_{-\mathbf{e}})|\mathbf{f} \notin t, u, v, w \text{ tree}\right] \leq 1.91$  where we used  $\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.001$ . therefore, using lemma 2.21, we get

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ u _ {t} + (a _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} \leq 1 \right] \geq 0. 4 9 \mathbb {p} \left[ u _ {t} + (a _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} \leq 1 | \mathbf {f} \notin t, u, v, w \text {t r e e} \right] \geq 0. 0 1,
$$

since  $\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.001$ , by lemma a.1,  $\mathbf{e}$  is 2-1-1 good.

![](images/917570795edfc4a5d8f21268fd6eae659b889d816375b2b3eaee7006f974e6a1.jpg)

![](images/14e3fdde946d151506a0534d6f7f465a4102e8b22a16e89153b424fc2b34ce63.jpg)  
figure 15: setting of lemma 5.24.

lemma 5.24. let  $\mathbf{e} = (\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})$  be a good half edge bundle and let  $a,b,c$  be the degree partitioning of  $\delta (u)$  (see fig. 15). if  $\epsilon_{1 / 2}\leq 0.001$  and  $x_{\mathbf{e}(a)},x_{\mathbf{e}(b)}\geq \epsilon_{1 / 2}$ , then

$$
\mathbb {p} [ \mathrm {e} 2 - 1 - 1 h a p p y w. r. t u ] \geq 0. 0 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} ^ {2}.
$$

proof. condition  $c_t$  to be zero,  $u, v$  and  $u \cup v$  be trees. this happens with probability at least 0.49. let  $\nu$  be the resulting measure. let  $x = a_{-\mathbf{e}} \cup b_{-\mathbf{e}}, y = \delta(v)_{-\mathbf{e}}$ . since  $\mathbf{e}$  is 2-2 good by lemma 5.15 and stochastic dominance,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ x _ {t} + y _ {t} \leq 2 \right] \geq \mathbb {p} \left[ (\delta (u) _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} + y _ {t} \leq 2 \right] - \mathbb {p} \left[ c _ {t} = 0 \right] \geq 0. 4 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} - 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 1} - \epsilon_ {\eta} \geq 0. 2 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2},
$$

where we used  $\epsilon_{1/1} < 12\epsilon_{1/2}$ . it follows by log-concavity of  $x_t + y_t$  that  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[x_t + y_t = 2] \geq 0.2\epsilon_{1/2}$ . now,

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ x _ {t} \right], \mathbb {e} _ {\nu} \left[ y _ {t} \right] \in \left[ 1 - 3 \epsilon_ {1 / 1}, 1. 5 + \epsilon_ {1 / 2} + 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 1} + 3 \epsilon_ {\eta} \right] \subset [ 0. 9 9 5, 1. 5 1 ]
$$

so,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ x _ {t} \geq 1 \right], \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ y _ {t} \geq 1 \right] \geq 0. 6 3, \tag {lemma2.22}
$$

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ x _ {t} \leq 1 \right], \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ y _ {t} \leq 1 \right] \geq 0. 2 4 5. \tag {markov}
$$

therefore, by corollary 5.5  $\mathbb{p}_{\nu}[x_t = 1|x_t + y_t = 2] \geq 0.119$ .

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\nu} \left[ x _ {t} = y _ {t} = 1 \right] \geq (0. 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}) 0. 1 1 9 \geq 0. 0 2 3 \epsilon_ {1 / 2},
$$

let  $\mathcal{e}$  be the event  $\{x_t = y_t = 1|\nu\}$ . note that in  $\nu$  we always choose exactly 1 edge from the e bundle and that is independent of edges in  $x, y$ , in particular the above event. therefore, we can correct the parity of  $a, b$  by choosing from  $e_a$  or  $e_b$ . it follows that

$$
\mathbb {p} [ \mathrm {e} 2 - 1 - 1 \text {h a p p y w . r . t} u ] \geq \mathbb {p} _ {\nu} [ \mathcal {e} ] (1. 9 9 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}) 0. 4 9 \geq 0. 0 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2} ^ {2},
$$

where we used that  $\mathbb{e}_{\nu}[\mathbf{e}(a)_t] \geq 1.99\epsilon_{1/2}$ , and the same fact for  $\mathbf{e}(b)_t$ . to see why this latter fact is true, observe that conditioned on  $u, v$  trees, we always sample at most one edge between  $u, v$ . therefore, since under  $\nu$  we choose exactly one edge between  $u, v$ , the probability of choosing from  $e(a)$  (and similarly choosing from  $\mathbf{e}(b)$ ) is at least

$$
\frac {\mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbf {e} (a) _ {t} | u , v \mathrm {t r e e s} , c _ {t} = 0 \right]}{\mathbb {p} \left[ \mathbf {e} | u , v \mathrm {t r e e s} , c _ {t} = 0 \right]} \geq \frac {x _ {\mathbf {e} (a)} - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}}{x _ {\mathbf {e}} + 3 \epsilon_ {1 / 1}} \geq \frac {\epsilon_ {1 / 2} - 2 \epsilon_ {\eta}}{1 / 2 + 1 . 3 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}} \geq 1. 9 9 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}
$$

as desired.

![](images/79d252f3398a17551f4cd9a0a2db50dcb0b9e69b1b231aa7f7e81f5d8812c577.jpg)

![](images/81b17a6d80ca4aa618572f36505bb58ee90968207de317f436afbe9141bb2906.jpg)  
figure 16: setting of lemma 5.27. we assume that the dotted green/blue edges are at most  $\epsilon_{1/2}$ . note that edges of  $c$  are not shown.

lemma 5.27. let  $\mathbf{e} = (\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}),\mathbf{f} = (\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})$  be two good top half edge bundles and let  $a,b,c$  be degree partitioning of  $\delta (v)$  such that  $x_{\mathbf{e}(b)},x_{\mathbf{f}(a)}\leq \epsilon_{1 / 2}$ . if  $\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}$  are not 2-1-1 good with respect to  $v$ , and  $\epsilon_{1 / 2}\leq 0.0002$ , then  $\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}$  are 2-2-2 happy with probability at least 0.01.

proof. first, observe that by lemma a.1 if  $\mathbb{p}\left[u_t + (a_{-\mathbf{e}})_t \leq 1\right] \geq 0.25\epsilon$ , where  $\epsilon \geq 20\epsilon_{1/2}$  is a constant that we fix later, then  $\mathbf{e}$  is 2-1-1 good, which is a contradiction. so, assume,  $\mathbb{p}\left[u_t + (a_{-\mathbf{e}})_t \geq 2\right] \geq 1 - 0.25\epsilon$ . furthermore, let  $q = \mathbb{p}\left[u_t + (a_{-\mathbf{e}})_t \geq 3\right]$ . since  $x(u) + x(a_{-\mathbf{e}}) \leq 2 + 3\epsilon_{1/2} + 2\epsilon_{1/1} + 3\epsilon_\eta \leq 2 + 3.2\epsilon_{1/2}$  (where we used  $x_{\mathbf{e}(a)} \geq x_{\mathbf{e}} - x_{\mathbf{e}(b)} - x_c \geq 1/2 - 2\epsilon_{1/2} - 2\epsilon_{1/1} - \epsilon_\eta$  and where we used  $12\epsilon_{1/1} \leq \epsilon_{1/2}$ ),

$$
2 (1 - q - 0. 2 5 \epsilon) + 3 q \leq 2 + 3. 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}.
$$

this implies that  $q \leq 0.5\epsilon + 3.2\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.75\epsilon$  (for  $\epsilon \geq 13\epsilon_{1/2}$ ). therefore,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ u _ {t} + (a _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} = 2 \right], \mathbb {p} \left[ w _ {t} + (b _ {- \mathbf {f}}) _ {t} = 2 \right] \geq 1 - \epsilon \tag {56}
$$

where the second inequality follows by a similar argument.

claim a.2. let  $z = \delta(u) \cap \delta(w)$ . if  $\epsilon < 1/15$ , then either  $\mathbb{e}[z|u, v, w \text{ tree}] \leq 3\epsilon$  or  $\mathbb{e}[z|u, v, w \text{ tree}] \geq (1 - 3\epsilon)$ .

proof. for the whole proof we work with  $\mu$  conditioned on  $u, v, w$  are trees. let  $z = \mathbb{e}[z]$ . let  $d = u \cup w \cup a_{-\mathbf{e}} \cup b_{-\mathbf{f}} \setminus z$ . note that  $d_t + 2z_t = u_t \cup w_t \cup (a_{-\mathbf{e}})_t \cup (b_{-\mathbf{f}})_t$ . by eq. (56) and a union bound  $\mathbb{p}[d_t + 2z_t = 4] \geq 1 - 2\epsilon - 3\epsilon_\eta$ . therefore,

$$
2. 1 \epsilon \geq 2 \epsilon + 3 \epsilon_ {\eta} \geq \mathbb {p} [ d _ {t} + 2 z _ {t} \neq 4 ] \geq \mathbb {p} [ d _ {t} = 3 ] \geq \sqrt {\mathbb {p} [ d _ {t} = 2 ] \mathbb {p} [ d _ {t} = 4 ]}
$$

where the last inequality follows by log-concavity. on the other hand,

$$
\begin{array}{l} z = \mathbb {p} [ z = 1 ] \leq \mathbb {p} [ d _ {t} = 2, z = 1 ] + \mathbb {p} [ d _ {t} + 2 z _ {t} \neq 4 ] \leq \mathbb {p} [ d _ {t} = 2 ] + 2. 1 \epsilon , \\ 1 - z = \mathbb {p} [ z = 0 ] \leq \mathbb {p} [ d _ {t} = 4, z = 0 ] + \mathbb {p} [ d _ {t} + 2 z _ {t} \neq 4 ] \leq \mathbb {p} [ d _ {t} = 4 ] + 2. 1 \epsilon \\ \end{array}
$$

putting everything together,

$$
(2. 1 \epsilon) ^ {2} \geq (z - 2. 1 \epsilon) (1 - z - 2. 1 \epsilon) = z (1 - z) - 2. 1 \epsilon + 2. 1 \epsilon^ {2}.
$$

therefore, using  $\epsilon \leq 1 / 15$ , we get that either  $z \leq 3\epsilon$  or  $z \geq 1 - 3\epsilon$ .

![](images/8381ba744b4b0aa921a6c330a982102e09c8a839b0d18c9daf1e8c313839d90d.jpg)

so, for the rest of proof we assume  $\mathbb{e}[z_t|u,v,w$  trees]  $< 3\epsilon$ . a similar proof shows  $\mathbf{e},\mathbf{f}$  are 2-2-2 good when  $\mathbb{e}[z_t|u,v,w$  trees]  $>1 - 3\epsilon$ . we run the following conditionings in order:  $u,v,w$  trees,  $z_{t} = 0$ ,  $c_t = 0$ ,  $\mathbf{e}(b)$ ,  $\mathbf{f}\notin t$ ,  $\mathbf{e}(a)\in t$ . note that  $\mathbf{e}(a)\in t$  is equivalent to  $u\cup v$  be a tree. call this event  $\mathcal{e}$  (i.e., the event that all things we conditioned on happen). first, notice

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ \mathcal {e} \right] \geq (1 - 3 \epsilon_ {\eta}) (1 - 3 \epsilon - 2 \epsilon_ {1 / 1} - \epsilon_ {\eta} - \epsilon_ {1 / 2} - (1 / 2 + \epsilon_ {1 / 2})) (1 / 2 - 3 \epsilon_ {1 / 2}) \geq 0. 2 2 \geq 1 / 5 \quad (5 7)
$$

moreover, since all of these conditionings correspond to upward/downward events,  $\mu |\mathcal{e}$  is strongly rayleigh. the main statement we will show is that

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ \mathbf {e}, \mathbf {f} 2 \text {-} 2 \text {-} 2 \text {h a p p y} | \mathcal {e} \right] \geq \mathbb {p} \left[ u _ {t} = (a _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} = 1, (b _ {- \mathbf {f}}) _ {t} = 0, w _ {t} = 2 | \mathcal {e} \right] = \omega (1).
$$

the main insight of the proof is that eq. (56) holds (up to a larger constant of  $\epsilon$ ), even after conditioning  $\mathcal{e}, b_{-\mathbf{f}} = 0, a_{-\mathbf{e}} = 1$ ; so, we can bound the preceding event by just a union bound. the main non-trivial statement is to argue that the expectations of  $b_{-\mathbf{f}}$  and  $a_{-\mathbf{e}}$  do not change so much under  $\mathcal{e}$ .

combining (56) and (57),

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ u _ {t} + (a _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} = 2 | \mathcal {e} \right], \mathbb {p} \left[ w _ {t} + (b _ {- \mathbf {f}}) _ {t} = 2 | \mathcal {e} \right] \geq 1 - 5 \epsilon . \tag {58}
$$

we claim that

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ b _ {t} | \mathcal {e} \right] = \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(b _ {- \mathbf {f}}\right) _ {t} | \mathcal {e} \right] \leq x \left(b _ {- \mathbf {f}}\right) + 3 \epsilon_ {\eta} + 3 \epsilon_ {1 / 1} + \epsilon_ {1 / 2} + 3 5 \epsilon \leq 0. 6 6 \tag {59}
$$

using  $\epsilon_{1/2} < 0.0002$  and  $\epsilon = 20\epsilon_{1/2}$ . to see this, observe that after each conditioning in  $\mathcal{e}$  either all marginals increase or all decrease. furthermore, the events  $c_t = 0, z_t = 0, \mathbf{e}(b)_t = 0$  can increase marginals by at most  $3\epsilon_\eta + 3\epsilon_{1/1} + \epsilon_{1/2}$ ; the only other event that can increase  $b_{-\mathbf{f}}$  is  $\mathbf{f} \notin t$ . now we know  $\mathbb{p}\left[(b_{-\mathbf{f}})_t + w_t = 2|\mathcal{e}\right] \geq 1 - 5\epsilon$  before and after conditioning  $\mathbf{f} \notin t$ . therefore, by corollary 2.19,  $2 - 10\epsilon \leq \mathbb{e}\left[(b_{-\mathbf{f}})_t + w_t\right] \leq 2 + 25\epsilon$ . but if  $\mathbb{e}\left[(b_{-\mathbf{f}})_t\right]$  increased by more than  $35\epsilon$ , then either before conditioning  $\mathbf{f} \notin t$ ,  $\mathbb{e}\left[(b_{-\mathbf{f}}) + w_t\right] < 2 - 10\epsilon$  or afterwards it is more than  $2 + 25\epsilon$ , which is a contradiction, and completes the proof of (59). a similar argument shows that  $\mathbb{e}\left[(a_{-\mathbf{e}})_t|\mathcal{e}\right] \leq 0.66$ .

we also claim that

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ (a _ {- e}) _ {t} | \mathcal {e} \right] \geq x (a _ {- e}) - 3 \epsilon_ {\eta} - 3 5 \epsilon \geq 0. 3 3.
$$

as above, everything conditioned on in  $\mathcal{e}$  increases  $\mathbb{e}\left[(a_{-e})_t\right]$  except for possibly  $\mathbf{e}(a)\in t$ . as above, we know that  $\mathbb{p}\left[u_t + (a_{-e})_t = 2|\mathcal{e}\right]\geq 1 - 5\epsilon$  before and after  $\mathbf{e}(a)\notin t$ . so again applying corollary 2.19, we see that it can't decrease by more than  $35\epsilon$ .

it follows that

$$
0. 3 3 \leq \mathbb {e} \left[ (a _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} | \mathcal {e} \right] \leq \mathbb {e} \left[ (a _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} | \mathcal {e}, (b _ {- \mathbf {f}}) _ {t} = 0 \right] \leq 0. 6 6 + 0. 6 6 \leq 1. 3 2.
$$

so, by lemma 2.21 and theorem 2.15,  $\mathbb{p}\left[(a_{-\mathbf{e}})_t = 1|\mathcal{e},(b_{-f})_t = 0\right]\geq 0.33e^{-.33}\geq 0.237.$

therefore, by lemma 2.21

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ \mathcal {e}, (a _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} = 1, (b _ {- \mathbf {f}}) _ {t} = 0 \right] \geq (0. 2 2) (0. 3 9) (0. 2 3) \geq 0. 0 1 9.
$$

therefore, by (58)

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ u _ {t} = 1 | \mathcal {e}, (a _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} = 1, (b _ {- \mathbf {f}}) _ {t} = 0 \right], \mathbb {p} \left[ w _ {t} = 2 | \mathcal {e}, (a _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} = 1, (b _ {- \mathbf {f}}) _ {t} = 0 \right] \geq 1 - 5 \epsilon / 0. 0 1 9
$$

finally, by union bound

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ u _ {t} = 1, w _ {t} = 2 | \mathcal {e}, (a _ {- \mathbf {e}}) _ {t} = 1, (b _ {- \mathbf {f}}) _ {t} = 0 \right] \geq 1 - \epsilon / 0. 0 0 9
$$

using  $\epsilon = 20\epsilon_{1/2}$  and  $\epsilon_{1/2} \leq 0.0002$  this means both of the above events happen, so  $\mathbf{e}, \mathbf{f}$  are 2-2-2-happy with probability  $0.019(1 - \epsilon / 0.009) > 0.01$  as desired.
